  EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
CATEGORICAL USE OF FORCE SYSTEMS AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Third Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
PURPOSE  
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year (FY) 2007/ 08 Annual Audit Plan, Audit Division (AD) 
conducted a Categorical Use of Force (CUOF) Sy stems Audit to evaluate compliance with  
14 Consent Decree (CD) Paragraphs (¶).  The aud it included a review of procedures that occur 
subsequent to a CUOF incident, such as officer separation, notif ication, and Behavioral Science 
Services referral.  
M
ETHODOLOGY  
 
Audit Division used five different populations to evaluate 14 CD ¶s, and made 18 compliance 
determinations.   
 
SUMMARY OF FINDINGS   
 
Each of the 18 compliance determinations met the 95 percent compliance standard and 
evidenced marked improvement from the prior y ear’s compliance rates.  Especially noteworthy 
was the improvement for the objective regardin g submission of CUOF reports to the Police 
Commission 60 days prior to statute.  Subsequent  to the actions taken by the Force Investigation 
Division (FID), performance increased in this area from 45 percent in the FY 2006/07 audit to  
97 percent in the FY 2007/08 audit.       
M
ANAGEMENT ’S RESPONSE  
 
On March 20, 2008, AD personnel met with the Commanding Officer (C/O) of FID  in order to 
discuss the findings of this audi t.  The FID C/O expressed genera l agreement with the findings.  
Due to the improvements and the consistent leve ls of compliance noted, Audit Division is not 
recommending any changes in process or procedure.  
 
The table on the following page indicates the compliance rates for the FY 2006/07 and  
FY 2007/08 audits.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
COMPLAINT , FORM 1.28, I NVESTIGATIONS AUDIT —P HASE II 
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Third Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
PURPOSE  
 
In accordance with the Annual Aud it Plan, Audit Division (AD) c onducted an audit of Complaint, 
Form 1.28, Investigations—Phase II pursuant to C onsent Decree (CD) Paragraphs (¶) 74(h), 75, 77, 
78, 80(a-g), 81, 82, 84, 85, 86, 90, 91, 101, and 129(b), (c), and (d).  
 
MANAGEMENT RESPONSE  
 
On March 24, 2008, AD met with the Commanding O fficer, Professional Standards Bureau (PSB), 
and discussed the findings.  Because of time lim itations related to Consent Decree requirements, a 
formal management response was not  obtained, but is forthcoming.   
 
FINDINGS  
 
The Department was non-compliant in eight of 32 obj ectives/sub-objectives for this audit.  This is a 
decrease from last year’s audit when the Depa rtment was not compliant on two objectives.  The 
decrease is due, in part, to a more rigorous methodology used by AD to more accurately measure its 
findings.  The objectives for which the Department did not meet the standards are summarized as 
follows:    
 
Three objectives (Objective Nos. 5[ b], [f], and [h]) invol ved PSB investigations for which witnesses 
or on-scene supervisors were not interviewed, or the scene was not canvassed to locate witnesses. 
 Two objectives (Objective Nos. 6[b] and [d]) i nvolved Chain of Command (COC) investigations for 
which on-scene supervisors were not interviewed or the scene was not canvassed for witnesses. 
  Three objectives involved investigations from bot h PSB and COC.  Three of the four objectives 
(Objective Nos. 14[c] and 15) decrea sed slightly from the prior audi t, causing the objectives to be 
non-compliant.  The remaining objective (Objective No. 13) decreased as a result of AD’s more 
rigorous methodology.  
 
It should also be noted that eigh t of the 32 objectives (Objective No s. 5[a], [g], and [i], 6[c], 7, 9, 
11, and 16) experienced an increase in compliance.  In two of these (Objec tive Nos. 5[g] and 16), 
the increase was significant enough to  cause the objective to go from non-compliant in last year’s 
audit to compliant in this audit. 
 
CONCLUSION  
 
The audit determined that adequate policies and procedures are in pla ce to effectively guide 
Department personnel through the inves tigation of complaints. The vast majority of errors identified 
in this audit were directly re lated to failures of investigators to follo w through with sound  
investigative practices.  Based on this audit’s findings, it appears that supervisors and management 
personnel should reinforce to subor dinates the importance of applyi ng these policies and procedures 
consistently and thoroughly.

ii 
 
 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
GANG AWARENESS TRAINING AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Third Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
PURPOSE  
 
This audit was conducted pursuant to the Los A ngeles Police Department’s Annual Audit Plan 
for Fiscal Year 2006/07.  The purpose of the audit was to evaluate the Department’s training of 
Special Enforcement Units (Gang Enforcement Details [GED]) as required by Consent Decree 
(CD) Paragraphs 106 and 106(a), which states the following: 
 
106.  The LAPD has developed and shall c ontinue to implement a protocol that 
includes the following requirements for managing and supervising all LAPD units that are 
primarily responsible for monitoring or reducing gang activity, in cluding the Special 
Enforcement Units:  
a. Each unit shall be assigned to an Ar ea or Bureau, and shall be managed and 
controlled by the Area or Bureau command staff where it is assigned. The Bureau gang coordinators and the citywide gang coor dinator (the Detective Support Division 
Commanding Officer) coordinate the Bureau -wide and citywide activities of these 
units, provide training and tec hnical assistance, and are i nvolved in coordinating and 
providing information for th e audits of these units. 
 
S
COPE AND METHODOLOGY  
 
The attendance of GED personnel at either a three-day Gang Awareness Course, or a one-day 
Gang Awareness Symposium, was used to evalua te compliance with Paragraph 106 and 106(a).  
Course rosters were examined to determine if  GED personnel attended training as required.  The 
course content was also evaluated in order to determine if course s provided training to the GEDs 
as required by the CD.  
 
FINDINGS  
 
The Department met the standards for this ob jective as 334 (96.5 percent) of 346 GED personnel 
attended the Gang Awareness Symposium in Oc tober 2007.  Additionally, as of November 2007, 
the three-day Gang Awareness Course was attended  by 48 percent of GED personnel.  The three-
day course is ongoing and can only accommodate fo rty officers at a time.  Future three-day 
courses are expected to be sc heduled for 2008.  Both the three- day training course and the Gang 
Awareness Symposium are post certified a nd focus on gang related issues.    
 
RECOMMENDATIONS  
 
Four recommendations were made by Gang a nd Operations Support Di vision.  The three-day 
Gang Awareness Course should continue to be offered to GED personnel.  The LAPD’s GED 
Tour Database should be monitored to ensure  attendance by GED personnel at the required 
training.  The Bureau Gang Coordinators should communicate with the Area Training 
Coordinator to ensure that GED personnel comp lete the necessary training.  Training should 
continue to be audited as part  of the Annual Audit Plan.   
 
 
 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
GANG ENFORCEMENT DETAIL SELECTION CRITERIA AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Third Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
PURPOSE    
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year (FY) 2007/08  Annual Audit Plan and pursuant to Consent 
Decree (CD) Paragraphs (¶) 51(b ), and (d) and 131(b), Audit Division (AD) conducted an audit 
to assess the Los Angeles Police Department’s (Department) compliance with the mandates set 
forth in CD ¶106(b), (c), and (d) and ¶107(a), (b), and (c).  Thes e paragraphs address eligibility 
and length of service restrictions for employees  assigned to Gang Enforcement Details (GED).  
They also set forth criteria for the sel ection and periodic reviews of officers  and supervisors 
during their GED tours.   
 This audit was conducted in accordance with gene rally accepted government auditing standards.  
Those standards require that the audit is planned and performed to  obtain sufficient, appropriate 
evidence to provide a reasonable basis for fi ndings and conclusions, based on the audit’s 
objectives.  Audit Division has determined that th e evidence obtained provides a reasonable basis 
for the findings and conclusions based on the audit objectives.  The work was limited to those areas specified in the “Scope and Methodology” a nd “Detailed Findings” sections of the audit. 
 
M
ETHODOLOGY AND AUDIT POPULATION  
 
The audit tested officers’ and supervisors’ selec tions into the GED, their continuing suitability 
for the positions, and whether extensions for limited a ssignments complied with CD mandates.         
 Audit Division also conducted ex tensive test work beyond the sc ope of the CD regarding the 
Department’s performance with certain policies and procedures. 
 Deployment Period (DP) 12, 2006 through DP 11, 2007 (November 12, 2006 through  November 10, 2007), was selected as the audit time period.    
S
UMMARY OF FINDINGS  
 
Consent Decree compliance increased from the pr ior audit for all objectives.  Objective  
No.1(d) – Evaluation of the Selection Process, was the only objective for which compliance was 
not met.  Findings associated with this objec tive related to missing documentation within GED 
selection packages.   The table on the following page illustrates th e Department’s compliance by audit objective, 
describes each objective as it re lates to the respective CD para graphs, and includes compliance 
percentages from the prior audit (FY 2006/07).  The audit measured the Department’s CD  
compliance level as a whole for each objective.  Therefore, findings are not appropriate for 
comparison of geographic Areas and Bureaus.

RECOMMENDATIONS  
 
The following recommendations were made due to the inconsistencies related to the GED 
Selection Checklists for new selections and extensions.  
 1. It is recommended that Pla nning and Research Division (PRD ) consider revising the GED  
Selection Checklist, New Selection, Form 12.16 .0; the GED Selection Checklist, Extension  
Request, Form 12.16.1; and, Special Or der No. 43, 2005, to be consistent. 
 2. It is recommended that PRD consider revi sing the GED Selection Checklist – Extension 
Request, Form 12.16.1, to be consistent with the GED Selection Checklist – New Selection, 
Form 12.16.0, by providing a space for the reviewer  to document his/her name and the date 
of review for Department personnel packag es, division employee folders, and PERs. 
 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
CATEGORICAL USE OF FORCE INVESTIGATIONS AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Fourth Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08 
 
 
PURPOSE  
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year (FY) 2007/08 A nnual Audit Plan, Audit Division (AD) conducted a 
Categorical Use of Force (CUOF) I nvestigations Audit.  The aud it evaluated compliance of CUOF 
investigations with Consen t Decree (CD) Paragraphs (¶) 57, 62, 80(a), (b), (c), (e), (f), and (g), 82, 
128, and 129.  
S
UMMARY OF FINDINGS   
 
Of the 23 compliance determinations, 20 met or exceeded the 95 percent compliance standard.  
Regarding the remaining three objectives: findings for two objectives (Objective Nos. 19 and 20) were 
administrative in nature; and, for the remaining object ive (Objective No. 17) ther e were four instances 
for which on-scene supervisors did not meet standards rega rding separation and monitoring of officers.           
 
Improvement in the compliance rate regarding thoroughness and co mpleteness of investigations 
(Objective No. 23) was noteworthy: fr om 81 percent in the prior audit to 100 percent in this audit.  
Increases in compliance determinations mainly were the result of strategies implemented by Force Investigation Division (FID).   
M
ANAGEMENT ’S RESPONSE  
 
On June 18, 2008, AD personnel met with the Commanding Officer (C/O) FID  in order to discuss the 
findings of this audit.  The C/O FID expressed general agreement with the findings.  Because of 
improvements and consistent levels of complia nce noted, AD did not recommend any changes in 
process or procedure.  
 
CONCLUSION  
 
Internal and external controls for each CUOF include multiple levels of review.  These controls are in 
place at FID, the Office of the Chief of Police, th e Office of the Inspector General, and the Police 
Commission.  These controls appear  to be effective as evidence d by reported compliance rates and 
year to year improvements.  Effective controls as well as risks should be taken into consideration when 
applying methodologies.  For future methodologies, Audit Division is co nsidering limited testing that 
would address both controls and risks regardi ng categorical uses of force.       
 The table on the following page indicates th e compliance rates for the FY 2006/07 and FY 2007/08 
audits.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
CONFIDENTIAL INFORMANT CONTROL PACKAGE AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Fourth Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08  
 
 
PURPOSE  
 
Pursuant to the Los Angeles Police Depart ment’s (Department) Annual Audit Plan, a 
confidential informant control package audit was completed.  The audit was conducted pursuant 
to Consent Decree (CD) Para graphs (¶) 108, 109, 128, and 131 (a ), (c), (d), and (e). 
 
AUDIT POPULATION  
 
Each of the Department’s informant control pack ages that were active at the time of the audit 
was reviewed.  The packages, which were cla ssified as Non-confidential Informant (NCI) 
packages, were not included in the determination of CD compliance.   
 
FINDINGS  
 
Of eighteen objectives tested, si xteen met the standards for each  objective for active informant 
packages.  For the two remaining objectives, the ma jority of the findings were attributable to 
inconsistencies between informant manuals.    
 
CONCLUSION  
 
The Department has been in substantial comp liance for the last two confidential informant 
audits.  There are multiple levels of internal cont rols in place to effectively manage and maintain 
informant packages.  In consider ation of best practices and th e two objectives for which 84 and 
87 percent compliance rates were reported, the fo llowing recommendations were made.       
 
1. Due to the low risk involved, it is recomme nded that Narcotics Divi sion (ND) revise the 
March 2008 Informant Manual, Section 220, Informants Requiring Special Review , to 
exclude the requirement for contacting th e court for CIs on summary probation.   
 
2. It is recommended that ND re vise Department Form 70-15.37.1- Narcotic Expenditure Secret 
Service Funds to include a space for the date of pre-approval.   
 
3. It is recommended that ND amend  the March 2008 Informant Manual, Section 250, to define 
“update” and delineate which forms shall be upd ated when a CI package is reactivated.   
 
4. It is recommended that the Exceptional Handling Report document the Commanding 
Officer’s consideration to use a criminal informant who has been convicted of perjury, 
assault with a deadly weapon on a po lice officer, or child molestation.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
 
NON-CATEGORICAL USE OF FORCE REPORTS AUDIT  
 
Conducted by Audit Division 
 
Fourth Quarter, Fiscal Year 2007/08  
 
PURPOSE  
 
In accordance with Fiscal Year (FY) 2007/08 A nnual Audit Plan, Audit Division (AD) conducted 
a Non-Categorical Use of Force (NCUOF) Reports Audit.   The audit evaluated compliance of 
NCUOF investigations/reports with Cons ent Decree (CD) Paragraphs (¶) 65, 68, 69,  
80(c), (e), and (f), 82, 128, 129(b), (c), and (d), and 131(a), (c), and (e).     
 
SUMMARY OF FINDINGS  
 
Of the 28 objectives measured, 12 did not meet the 95 percent compliance standard.  However, 
eight of the 12 non-compliant objectives were 90 percent compliant or higher, with significant 
increases in compliance percentages in most areas.   As in the prior year’s audit, findings were 
especially noted in two areas and appear to be resolvable through training.  Audit Division 
recommended that all supervisors who conduct or  review NCUOF incide nts receive training on 
the proper investigative and re view process.  These two obj ectives are detailed below.  
 
Objective No. 4(b) – Watch Commander Insight  
Deficiencies were identified in watch co mmanders’ assessments of officers’ actions, 
including insufficient insight into the incident .  It appears the watch commanders continue 
to misconstrue their responsibilities in asse ssing officer actions in the Watch Commander 
Insight on the Internal Progre ss Report.   Each finding ei ther lacked the specificity 
necessary to determine which officer actions we re assessed, or applicat ions of force used 
by the officers were not addressed.   
  
Objective No. 9(e) – Adequ acy of the Investigation  
Deficiencies were identified regarding photograp hing officer injuries, or lack thereof, in 
cases for which the primary or requested filing charge was for interfering with, or resisting 
of, a police officer.  Twenty-six cases involved documented injuries to officers that that 
were not photographed by a supervisor, or th e photographs were insufficient to document 
the injury.   
OTHER RELATED MATTER – TIMELY COMPLETION OF NCUOF I NVESTIGATIONS  
 
As in the prior audit, the methodology for obtaini ng NCUOF reports was alte red as a result of the 
untimely closure of cases.  Since December 2005, the Department processes NCUOF incidents via 
an automated system (TEAMS II).   Although TEAMS II has made significant improvements on 
the technical aspects, investigative and administ rative review delays continue to plague the 
process.  During September 2007, 13 3 NCUOF investigations were in itiated.  As of April 30, 2008 
(seven months later), only two had been comple ted (i.e., reviewed and in “Admin” status by the 
Commanding Officer, Use of Force Review Divi sion.  Audit Division will conduct a supplemental 
audit during the first quarter of  FY 2008/09 to determine the cause of these delays.      
 
ii

OFFICE OF THE CHIEF OF POLICE 
 
 
SPECIAL ORDER NO. 7     February 14, 2008 
 
APPROVED BY THE BOARD OF POLICE COMMISSIONERS ON JANUARY 29, 2008 
 
SUBJECT:  ORGANIZATIONAL PERFORMANCE ASSESSMENTS  
 
PURPOSE:  The City and the Department have established TEAMS II  
to contain relevant information about its personnel to 
promote professionalism and best policing practices and to 
recognize exceptional behavior or identify and modify at-risk 
behavior.  As part of that process, this Order implements the 
protocols for monitoring the performance of Department units and 
the Department as a whole as part of a larger strategic and 
organizational risk assessment process.   
It is the responsibility of the Department to regularly review 
the performance of organizational entities Department-wide.  
Regular performance assessments provide tools to assist in 
predicting future performance and to allow supervisors and 
managers to either reward exceptional performance or initiate 
appropriate monitoring strategies.  The Consent Decree requires a regular review by appropriate managers of all relevant  
TEAMS II information to evaluate the performance of Department 
entities in order to identify any patterns or series of 
incidents that may indicate at-risk behavior.  The assessment 
will include an independent review, conclusions, and 
recommendations to improve performance and accountability and to 
facilitate decision-making. 
 
 
PROCEDURE :  The Risk Management Executive Committee (RMEC) shall 
 conduct the following: 
  
 *  Establish policies and guidelines for completing regular  
    and periodic Organizational Performance  Assessments   
    (OPA); and, 
*  Review and maintain OPA to evaluate and identify any  
   patterns or series of incidents that indicate at-risk   
   behavior as part of a strategic risk management process.  
 
Note:  Every commanding officer shall cooperate fully with 
RMEC or its designee in completing OPA according to the 
policies, guidelines and schedule established and/or approved 
by RMEC. 
 
AMENDMENTS:   This Order amends Section 2/093.95 of the 
Department Manual.  
SPECIAL ORDER NO. 7 -2- February 14, 2008 
 
 
 
AUDIT RESPONSIBILITY:  The Commanding Officer, Risk Management 
Group, shall monitor compliance with this directive in 
accordance with Department Manual Section 0/080.30.  
  
 
 
WILLIAM J. BRATTON 
Chief of Police

SUBJECT:  PERFORMANCE EVALUATI ON REPORT – CAPTAINS AND ABOVE,  
 FORM 01.33.00; PERFORMANCE EVALUATION REPORT – LIEUTENANTS 
AND BELOW, FORM 01.78.00; AND TEAMS EVALUATION REPORT,  
 FORM 01.78.04 - REVISED  
 
 
Pursuant to Consent Decree mandates and Special Order No. 22, “Duty to Conduct a nd 
Document Individual Performance Assessments,” dated July 12, 2007, a supervisor is required to 
create a Supervisor Action Item (SAI) in the Risk Management Information System (RMIS).  A 
SAI shall be generated to document reviews for the following:  
 
* Annual performance evaluation;  
* Pay grade advancement;  
* Assignment to Gang Enforcement Details, Force Investigation Division, Professional 
Standards Bureau, and Field Training Officer positions;  
* Employee transfers to new assignments; or,  
* Periodic monit oring.   
 
To document this process, the following three forms have been revised to include a field where a 
supervisor shall enter the RMIS Action Item Number:  
 
* Performance Evaluation Report – Captains and Above, Form 01.33.00;  
* Performance Evaluation Re port – Lieutenants and Below, Form 01.78.00; and,  
* TEAMS Evaluation Report, Form 01.78.04.  
 
These revised forms are immediately available at LAPDFORMS on the Department’s Local 
Area Network.  Any questions regarding this Notice may be directed to  
Captain  Carol J. Aborn Khoury, Commanding Officer, Planning and Research Division, at  
(213) 485 -4111.  
 
 
 
 
WILLIAM J. BRATTON  
Chief of Police  
 
DISTRIBUTION “D”  
  OFFICE OF OPERATIONS 
 
      
 OPERATIONS NOTICE NO. 6                      March 18, 2008  
 
SUBJECT: CLARIFICATION OF BOOKING APPROVAL PROCEDURES 
 
EFFECTIVE:  IMMEDIATELY 
 
A recent review of the Department’s booking ap proval process uncovered a conflict between a 
Detective Notice, dated July 18, 2003, and Depa rtment Manual Section 4/216.01, which reads as 
follows:    
“Booking approval for any arrest shall only be obtained from an Area watch  
commander or the Watch Commander Metropo litan Jail Section, Jail Division.  When 
providing booking approval, the watch comm ander shall review each arrest for 
appropriateness, legality, and conformance with Department policy and procedure.” 
 Department Manual Section 4/216.01 is the book ing approval procedure that Department 
employees shall follow; therefore,  this Notice serves as a reminde r to Department personnel that 
booking approval shall be obtained from the uniformed Area watch commander or the uniformed Watch Commander Metropolitan Jail Section, Jail Division.  
 Booking advice shall still be obtained from an  on-duty investigative supervisor from the 
investigative entity responsible for the follow-up investigation.  Booking a dvice is not required if 
the investigative enti ty responsible for the follow-up investiga tion is off-duty, nor is it required if 
the arrest is narcotics related and no fo llow-up investigation will be conducted. 
 
Any questions regardi ng this Notice s hould be directed to the Evaluation and Admi nistration Section, 
Office of Operations , at (213) 473-8826. 
    EARL C. PAYSINGER, Assistant Chief Director, Office of Operations  DISTRIBUTION “D”
